 Every deploy answers `201 Created` with a small JSON object (`verb`, `kind`, `sha256`, `imports`), which the tutorial does not repeat. A new verb is private to its owner. `exec=public` lets anyone call it. Normally a verb runs with its caller’s authority and so cannot write an object the caller does not own, which would stop Bob from bumping Alice’s counter. `runs_as=owner` makes this one verb run with its owner’s authority instead. That is a deliberate widening you opt into per verb; the default stays run-as-the-caller. Bob and an anonymous caller can both click: 
```sh
curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $BOB" localhost:8080/v1/links/rust-book:visit
curl -X POST localhost:8080/v1/links/rust-book:visit
```
 
```json
{"url":"https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/","clicks":1}
{"url":"https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/","clicks":2}
```
 The count is now part of the object’s data, readable with a plain `GET`: 
```sh
curl localhost:8080/v1/links/rust-book
```
 
```json
{"clicks":2,"tag":"rust","title":"The Rust Book","url":"https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/"}
```
 ## 5. Search the collection A function bound to `links` can read every object under the prefix `links/` in one call with `obj.query`. It returns an ordered, filtered page of objects, limited to what the caller may read: 
```sh
cat > search.js <<'EOF'
// links:search  input: {"tag": "rust"} (optional); returns the links under links/
function invoke(input) {
  const opts = input ? JSON.parse(input) : {};
  const q = { prefix: "links/", orderBy: "title", limit: 50 };
  if (opts.tag) q.predicate = { key: "tag", value: opts.tag };
  const page = obj.query(q);
  return page.objects.map(o => ({
    id: o.path.slice("links/".length),
    title: o.properties.title,
    url: o.properties.url,
    tag: o.properties.tag,
    clicks: o.properties.clicks ?? 0,
  }));
}
EOF
curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $ALICE" --data-binary @search.js \
  'localhost:8080/v1/modules?verb=links:search&kind=function&lang=text/javascript&exec=public'
```
 It is a function because it only reads, so you call it with `GET`: 
```sh
curl localhost:8080/v1/links:search
```
 
```json
[{"id":"rust-book","title":"The Rust Book","url":"https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/","tag":"rust","clicks":2}]
```
 `orderBy` and `predicate` run in the store. A page holds at most 1000 objects, and a longer result carries a `cursor` to pass back as `cursor` in the next call. With this many links one page is enough. ## 6. Let users add links as themselves A direct `PUT` stores whatever the client sends and records no author. A verb can validate the input and stamp the caller’s name on the link. Inside a verb, `caller.subject()` is the authenticated identity, and `obj.setAt` writes to another object *as the caller*: 
```sh
cat > add.js <<'EOF'
// links:add  input: {"id","title","url","tag"}; records who added the link
function invoke(input) {
  const l = JSON.parse(input);
  if (!l.id || !l.url) throw new Error("id and url are required");
  const path = "links/" + l.id;
  obj.setAt(path, "title", l.title ?? l.id);
  obj.setAt(path, "url", l.url);
  obj.setAt(path, "tag", l.tag ?? "misc");
  obj.setAt(path, "addedBy", caller.subject() ?? "anonymous");
  return { added: path };
}
EOF
curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $ALICE" --data-binary @add.js \
  'localhost:8080/v1/modules?verb=links:add&kind=action&lang=text/javascript&exec=public'
```
 This verb does not set `runs_as=owner`, so it runs as whoever calls it. Bob adds two links: 
```sh
curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $BOB" \
  --data '{"id":"axum","title":"Axum","url":"https://docs.rs/axum","tag":"rust"}' \
  localhost:8080/v1/links:add


curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $BOB" \
  --data '{"id":"docs-rs","title":"docs.rs","url":"https://docs.rs","tag":"tools"}' \
  localhost:8080/v1/links:add
```
 
```json
{"added":"links/axum"}
{"added":"links/docs-rs"}
```
 Bob now owns those two objects. Because the verb runs as Bob, it cannot be used to overwrite Alice’s link: 
```sh
curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $BOB" \
  --data '{"id":"rust-book","url":"https://evil.example"}' \
  localhost:8080/v1/links:add
```
 
```json
{"detail":"'bob' may not write 'links/rust-book'","restact_code":"forbidden","restact_verb":":add","status":403,"title":"Forbidden","type":"about:blank"}
```
 The same write check that blocked Bob’s direct `PUT` in step 3 applies inside the verb. A verb can never do more than its caller. See [Identity and authority](/docs/concepts/identity-and-authority/). Search again, filtering on a tag. `input` is a query parameter; `--data-urlencode` with `-G` encodes it: 
```sh
curl -G --data-urlencode 'input={"tag":"rust"}' localhost:8080/v1/links:search
```
 
```json
[{"id":"axum","title":"Axum","url":"https://docs.rs/axum","tag":"rust","clicks":0},{"id":"rust-book","title":"The Rust Book","url":"https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/","tag":"rust","clicks":2}]
```
 ## 7. React to new links with a hook A lifecycle hook is a verb with a reserved name that the host calls after a write. `onCreate` runs when an object is first written. Keep a `feed` object holding the most recent addition. Alice creates the object and deploys the hook: 
```sh
curl -X PUT -H "Authorization: Bearer $ALICE" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  --data '{"title":"Latest link"}' localhost:8080/v1/feed


cat > oncreate.js <<'EOF'
// links/{id}:onCreate  runs after a new link object is first written
function invoke(event) {
  obj.setAt("feed", "latest", event);
  return "ok";
}
EOF
curl -g -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $ALICE" --data-binary @oncreate.js \
  'localhost:8080/v1/modules?verb=links/{id}:onCreate&kind=action&lang=text/javascript'
```
 A hook runs with its owner’s authority, not the writer’s. That is why Alice’s hook can write to `feed`, which Bob does not own. The writer’s identity arrives in the event payload for information only. Bob adds one more link: 
```sh
curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $BOB" \
  --data '{"id":"tokio","title":"Tokio","url":"https://tokio.rs","tag":"rust"}' \
  localhost:8080/v1/links:add
curl localhost:8080/v1/feed
curl localhost:8080/v1/links/tokio
```
 
```json
{"added":"links/tokio"}
{"latest":"{\"event\":\"onCreate\",\"object\":\"links/tokio\",\"subject\":\"bob\"}","title":"Latest link"}
{"addedBy":"bob","tag":"rust","title":"Tokio","url":"https://tokio.rs"}
```
 Hooks are synchronous and best-effort: if a hook fails, the write that triggered it is kept. See [Lifecycle hooks](/docs/guides/lifecycle-hooks/). ## What you built | Piece | Where it is used | | ----------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- | | Object data over `PUT` and `GET` | Links, with the first writer owning each one | | An action bound to `links/{id}` | `visit`, using `obj.get` and `obj.set` | | A function over a collection | `search`, using `obj.query` | | Authentication and run-as-caller | `add`, where Bob’s identity limits what the verb can write | | A per-verb opt-out of run-as-caller | `visit`, with `runs_as=owner` | | A lifecycle hook | `onCreate`, feeding the `feed` object | ## Where to go next * Run a verb on a timer, for example to purge stale links: [Schedules and subscriptions](/docs/guides/schedules/). * Keep API keys out of verbs and route model calls through a connector: [Outbound calls and secrets](/docs/guides/egress/). * Call one verb from another: [Compose verbs](/docs/guides/composition/). * Replace the shared secret with your identity provider: [Add authentication](/docs/guides/authentication/). * Everything about objects, ownership and queries: [Store and query object data](/docs/guides/object-data/). * Before you expose this to anyone else: [Production checklist](/docs/operate/production/).
